The test `test_export_appends_totals_row` expects that when called with `totals=True`, it should include a totals row. Currently, `export_csv` doesn't accept a `totals` parameter. We need to modify `export_csv` to optionally include a totals row when `totals=True` is passed.\n\nWe also have `column_totals` function to compute totals, and `render_rows` to render rows. Perhaps we should modify `export_csv` to check if `totals` is True, then compute the totals row and append it.\n\nLooking at the expected output: `\"1,2\\n3,4\\nTOTAL,4,6\"`. The totals row starts with \"TOTAL\" followed by the column totals. So we need to render a row where the first element is \"TOTAL\" and the rest are the column totals.\n\nWe have `column_totals(rows)` that returns [4,6] for the example. So we could do:\n\n```python\ndef export_csv(rows, totals=False):\n    rendered = render_rows(rows)\n    if totals:\n        totals_row = [\"TOTAL\"] + column_totals(rows)\n        rendered.append(render_rows([totals_row])[0])  # render_rows expects a list of rows, returns list of strings\n        # or just do \",\".join(str(v) for v in [\"TOTAL\"] + column_totals(rows))\n    return \"\\n\".join(rendered)\n```\n\nBut we need to be careful: `render_rows` takes a list of rows and returns a list of strings. If we pass `[totals_row]`, it will return a list with one string. If we do `rendered = render_rows(rows)`, that's a list of strings for the data rows. Then we append the rendered totals row string. Then join with newlines.\n\nAlternatively, we could do:\n\n```python\n    if totals:\n        totals_row_values = column_totals(rows)\n        rendered.append(\"TOTAL,\" + \",\".join(str(v) for v in totals_row_values))\n```\n\nBut using `render_rows` ensures consistent formatting (comma joining). However, `render_rows` uses `str(v)` for each value, which is fine. For the \"TOTAL\" string, we need to make sure it's converted to string. `render_rows([[\"TOTAL\"] + totals_row_values])` would work if `totals_row_values` are numbers, but \"TOTAL\" is a string.
